NOT for single-cell RNA-seq, differential expression testing, methylation analysis, or datasets that are too small for WGCNA after quality control. - **wgcna-co-expression-module-detection**: Use when input is a processed single-cell expression matrix with cell-level metadata and the goal is to infer co-expression modules and identify which modules track a trait such as patient survival status. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/wgcna-co-expression-module-detection--6061abef195e5222))
- **wgcna-co-expression-module-detection**: Use when you have an RNA-seq table of counts plus sample phenotype metadata (e.g., mutant/wild-type Lafora disease samples) and want to find co-expressed gene groups (CEGs) that correlate with phenotypic data. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/wgcna-co-expression-module-detection--0b3b74c66789bebb))
- **wgcna-coexpression-network-inference**: Use when the input is a preprocessed single-cell expression matrix (e.g. .h5ad/HDF5/loom) with cell metadata and the goal is to infer gene co-expression modules and hubs for specific cell types or conditions. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/wgcna-coexpression-network-inference))
- **wgcna-comparative-module-inference**: Use when a co-expression/co-abundance matrix (e.g. genus-level counts from QIIME 2) must be partitioned into modules by both SGCRNA and WGCNA to compare module granularity and preprocessing retention. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-multi-omics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-multi-omics/tree/HEAD/collections/multi-omics/v1/leaves/wgcna-comparative-module-inference))
- **wgcna-gene-module-detection**: Use when a cell-type-specific co-expression matrix (e.g., from CS-CORE or diff_CSCORE) needs to be partitioned into co-expressed gene modules for downstream GO enrichment of disease-relevant pathways. - **wgcna-module-constraint**: Build and audit bulk WGCNA modules, module-trait relationships, hub-gene constraints, and downstream module handoffs. Use when an expression matrix needs co-expression modules or when a downstream analysis must be constrained to stable modules. - **wgcna-module-detection**: Use when you have a metabolite-by-sample quantification matrix (e.g. LC-MS peak areas from a pseudotargeted metabolomics run) and need to group co-accumulating metabolites into modules for biomarker metabolite screening. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/wgcna-module-detection))
- **wgcna-module-detection**: Use when you have a bulk RNA-seq count matrix across many samples (e.g. 38 female hippocampus 3xTg-AD/WT samples) and need to find co-expressed gene modules and correlate them with external traits. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/wgcna-module-detection))
- **wgcna-module-detection-metabolomics**: Use when a normalized metabolite measurement table (e.g. Metabolon®-style data with SUB_PATHWAY annotations) should be partitioned into co-abundance modules and tested against sample traits such as tumor vs normal status or gender, without predefined metabolite sets. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/wgcna-module-detection-metabolomics))
- **wgcna-module-eigenmetabolite-screening**: Use when analyzing a pseudotargeted metabolomics feature/peak-area table (metabolites × samples) across a mutant or treatment panel to find co-abundance modules and rank samples by module eigenmetabolite for biomarker metabolite screening. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabolomics/tree/HEAD/collections/metabolomics/v3/leaves/wgcna-module-eigenmetabolite-screening))
- **wgcna-module-richness-characterization**: Use when WGCNA subnetworks (e.g. seasonal 'grey' and 'blue' modules) from a Monterey Bay-style eDNA co-occurrence network must be characterized by taxon richness and correlated against environmental variables such as chlorophyll a or sea surface temperature. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabarcoding](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-metabarcoding/tree/HEAD/collections/metabarcoding/v1/leaves/wgcna-module-richness-characterization))
- **wgcna-module-trait-association-testing**: Use when a PyWGCNA co-expression network has already been built and you need module–trait correlations (e.g., module eigengene vs. genotype or age/time point) plus GO/KEGG/REACTOME enrichment of the correlated module. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-transcriptomics/tree/HEAD/collections/transcriptomics/v2/leaves/wgcna-module-trait-association-testing))
- **wgcna-network-query**: 通过线上 Potato Agent WGCNA API 查询马铃薯 DMv8.2 基因的网络归属、模块、kME、TOM 排名共表达邻居、跨网络模块重叠和共享共表达边，并生成与 Interface WGCNA 查看器视觉语义一致的 SVG 或 PDF 矢量网络图。用于回答 leaf、stem、root、reproductive 或 tuberization 网络中的 WGCNA、共表达、候选 hub gene、模块成员、跨组织网络保守性和网络绘图问题。 ([biojiayuxin/potato-agent](https://github.com/biojiayuxin/potato-agent/tree/HEAD/skills/potato-knowledge-bioinformatics/wgcna-network-query) · ★1)

- **wgcna-scale-free-parameter-tuning**: Use when a co-expression analysis must assume a scale-free topology and needs the R WGCNA package run with manually tuned parameters (soft-thresholding power) on a gene-by-sample count matrix, e.g. as a baseline for comparison with scale-free-free methods like SGCRNA. ([HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-multi-omics](https://github.com/HolobiomicsLab/asb-skills-multi-omics/tree/HEAD/collections/multi-omics/v1/leaves/wgcna-scale-free-parameter-tuning))

Covers the draft to connect lifecycle, the manifest/spec/schema artifacts, where every key nests, authentication shapes, the connected account and its credentials, source discovery, report types, pagination, and the guards that restrict what a stored definition may contain. Use when asked to add or connect a data source Whatagraph does not already support, or to fix, extend, or re-sync one that was built this way. ([whatagraph/whatagraph-skills](https://github.com/whatagraph/whatagraph-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/whatagraph-dynamic-integrations))
- **whatagraph-export**: Get something out of a report — a rendered PDF, an Excel (.xlsx) download, inline widget data, page images you can look at yourself, or the actual ad-creative images from its media widgets. Use when a user wants a PDF or a spreadsheet, when you need the raw rows, when you must see how a report renders before you call it finished, or when the task is about how the ads themselves look (creative analysis, creative QA). ([whatagraph/whatagraph-skills](https://github.com/whatagraph/whatagraph-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/whatagraph-export))
- **whatagraph-filters**: Create saved filter configurations for a channel. Filters are per-channel conditions on a dimension or metric that can be attached to widgets or reports via the UI. Use when a user wants reusable "only this audience / only this campaign / only above $X" filtering. ([whatagraph/whatagraph-skills](https://github.com/whatagraph/whatagraph-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/whatagraph-filters))
- **whatagraph-freeform-reports**: Build freeform reports — documents where a slide deck, a printable client report and a continuous dashboard are one type in three formats. Blocks are positioned on the page and named by theme role, never by colour. Use when someone wants a deck, a presentation, a designed or printable report, a one-pager, or a dashboard view, rather than a grid report. Also covers design systems (brands) — the six decisions a freeform report is drawn in. ([whatagraph/whatagraph-skills](https://github.com/whatagraph/whatagraph-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/whatagraph-freeform-reports))
- **whatagraph-goals**: Create goals — targets on metrics (monthly spend caps, lead targets, ROAS floors) with time periods and optional dimension filtering. Use when a user wants to track progress toward a number by a deadline. ([whatagraph/whatagraph-skills](https://github.com/whatagraph/whatagraph-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/whatagraph-goals))
- **whatagraph-integrations-admin**: Browse available integrations, connect sources from already-authenticated accounts, and assign sources to spaces. Use when onboarding a new client (adding their sub-accounts into Whatagraph) or when reallocating existing sources across client folders. ([whatagraph/whatagraph-skills](https://github.com/whatagraph/whatagraph-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/whatagraph-integrations-admin))
- **whatagraph-mcp-overview**: Orientation skill for the Whatagraph MCP server. Read first when working on any Whatagraph task. Explains the mental model (spaces → reports → tabs → widgets; data sources, source groups, blends; overviews; templates) and routes to the right specialized skill — both the configuration skills and the read-only analysis/insight skills (fetching metrics, report digests, marketing insights, account-health audits, troubleshooting). ([whatagraph/whatagraph-skills](https://github.com/whatagraph/whatagraph-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/whatagraph-mcp-overview))
- **whatagraph-offline-reports**: Build a complete Whatagraph report from numbers you already have, with no connected data source. Use when the user says "here is my analysis, make me a report", pastes figures, points at a spreadsheet or document, or asks for a deck-style leadership or board report that Whatagraph cannot pull by itself. Covers shaping external numbers into offline widgets, designing the page with text widgets, and the render limits that silently spoil a finished PDF. ([whatagraph/whatagraph-skills](https://github.com/whatagraph/whatagraph-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/whatagraph-offline-reports))
- **whatagraph-overviews**: Create overviews — KPI dashboards that track metrics over time. The UI calls these "Measurements". Use when a user wants a single-page KPI dashboard with trend visualizations and comparisons, independent of any report. Caution — "overview" in a prompt often means a report tab or a summary report instead of this entity; load this skill to run the disambiguation check (and ask the user a follow-up) whenever the meaning isn't clear from context. ([whatagraph/whatagraph-skills](https://github.com/whatagraph/whatagraph-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/whatagraph-overviews))
- **whatagraph-report-tabs**: Create, duplicate, rename, and reorder tabs within a report, and open or close rows in a tab's grid. Each tab is a page of widgets. Use when a report needs a new section (e.g. "Paid Search", "Social", "Organic"), when existing tabs need to be re-ordered or duplicated, when a widget has to go between two existing rows, or when a tab has blank rows to close up. Handles asks like "add a new page to this report", "rename the second tab", "reorder the report sections", "insert a row between these two widgets", or "remove the empty rows / close the gaps / compact the tab". ([whatagraph/whatagraph-skills](https://github.com/whatagraph/whatagraph-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/whatagraph-report-tabs))
- **whatagraph-reports**: Create, duplicate, and update reports. Reports live in spaces and contain one or more tabs of widgets. Use when a user wants a new client deliverable, to clone an existing report, or to bulk-swap the data sources a report points at. ([whatagraph/whatagraph-skills](https://github.com/whatagraph/whatagraph-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/whatagraph-reports))
- **whatagraph-sharing**: Create, update and revoke public share links for reports, overviews and documents (files an agent made, such as a web page, deck or PDF), with optional password protection. Use when a user wants to give a client a view-only link, lock the date range viewers see, turn AI chat on inside a shared view, or share a document from the chat. For a PDF or a spreadsheet, load `whatagraph-export` instead — getting a file out of a report is not sharing. ([whatagraph/whatagraph-skills](https://github.com/whatagraph/whatagraph-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/whatagraph-sharing))
- **whatagraph-snapshots**: Save (create) and restore the structural state of a report — tabs, widgets, layout. Use when a user wants a checkpoint before a risky edit, to revert to a prior version, or to view snapshot history. Handles asks like "save a backup of this report before I change it", "undo my changes and go back to the previous version", or "show me this report's version history". ([whatagraph/whatagraph-skills](https://github.com/whatagraph/whatagraph-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/whatagraph-snapshots))
- **whatagraph-source-groups**: Sum many data sources into one virtual aggregated source — same-channel (e.g. five Google Ads sub-accounts) or cross-channel (e.g. Meta + Google + Reddit + TikTok). Scales to dozens or hundreds of sources, where a blend would need a join per extra source. Use when an agency wants unified reporting without building a blend. ([whatagraph/whatagraph-skills](https://github.com/whatagraph/whatagraph-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/whatagraph-source-groups))
- **whatagraph-sources-and-data**: Discover and query Whatagraph data sources — find which accounts are connected, list report types and the available metrics and dimensions (narrowing large catalogs with the filter parameter), pull raw numbers via fetch-data, and check where a source is used. Covers native channels, source groups, and blends. Use this before any reporting task to establish what data is available. ([whatagraph/whatagraph-skills](https://github.com/whatagraph/whatagraph-skills/tree/HEAD/skills/whatagraph-sources-and-data))
